**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Agent Lang states that Judge Gavèlle came back down on the elevator alone and there was no sign of President Wang getting on the elevator at all. This implies that President Wang did not use the elevator to leave the roof. However, Evidence 10 (Security Camera Footage) shows President Wang and Judge Gavèlle in front of the elevator on the roof of the Bigg Building, but there's no footage of the president riding the elevator down to leave. This suggests that President Wang could have potentially left the roof via another means, such as being pushed off, which contradicts Lang's implication that the only way down was the elevator.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

None of the spatial propositions directly address the specific scenario of President Wang leaving the roof without using the elevator.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Since no spatial propositions directly apply, we skip this step.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial context of the roof of the Bigg Building and the only known exit route, the elevator. Lang's testimony suggests that the elevator was the only way down, yet the security camera footage does not show President Wang using the elevator, indicating that there might be another way he could have left the roof, such as being pushed off.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 10, "testimony": 3}

--------------------
{
  "evidence": 10,
  "testimony": 3

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

Upon reviewing the provided evidences and testimonies, we need to look for inconsistencies in their descriptions regarding location and presence. Evidence 16 states that the Jammin' Ninja's Button was found in Engarde's hakama and is covered in Corrida's blood. Testimony 3 confirms this finding but does not mention the blood covering the button.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

We see that Proposition 1 directly applies to the Jammin' Ninja's Button being found in Engarde's hakama.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If the Jammin' Ninja's Button is found in Engarde's hakama, then it is covered by Engarde's hakama."

This proposition implies that the button would be covered by Engarde's hakama if found within it. However, the critical detail here is the blood covering the button. If the button was covered by Engarde's hakama, it would likely not have visible blood stains unless the hakama itself was stained with blood, which is not mentioned in the testimonies or evidences.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises because the description of the Jammin' Ninja's Button being covered in Corrida's blood suggests that it was exposed to the environment where the blood was present (the crime scene). If the button had been covered by Engarde's hakama, it would not have been visibly covered in blood as described in the evidence.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 16, "testimony": 3}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 16,
  "testimony": 3
}

## OBJECTIVE:
**FIND THE CONTRADICTION:** Which evidence and testimony contradict each other?

## CONSTRAINT:
**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Kay mentions that she feels like she saw something red from under the cherry tree (Testimony 3). According to Evidence 7, the Bigg Building Pamphlet, the cherry tree is located to the left-most edge of the viewing platform. This suggests that Kay was facing the cherry tree when she saw the red object.

However, Kay also recalls that someone was walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand (Testimony 4). Evidence 7 states that the candy stand is located to the right-most edge of the viewing platform. This implies that Kay was facing the candy stand when she was pushed.

These two statements create a spatial contradiction because Kay cannot simultaneously be facing both the left-most cherry tree and the right-most candy stand.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1 and 2 are relevant to this analysis. Proposition 1 deals with the position relative to the candy stand, while Proposition 2 deals with the position relative to the cherry tree.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if Kay was pushed off the Bigg Building from beyond the candy stand, then the person pushing her was to the right of Kay. This aligns with Testimony 4 where Kay says someone was walking towards her from the other side of the candy stand.

Proposition 2 states that if Kay felt like she saw something red from under the cherry tree, then the red object was to the left of Kay. This aligns with Testimony 3 where Kay feels like she saw something red from under the cherry tree.

Since Kay cannot be facing both the left and the right simultaneously, these two propositions highlight the contradiction in her testimonies.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the spatial orientation described in Kay's testimonies. She cannot be facing both the left-most cherry tree and the right-most candy stand at the same time. Therefore, her recollection of seeing something red from under the cherry tree (left) and being pushed from beyond the candy stand (right) cannot both be true in the context of her facing one direction.

**Step 5:** Provide your answer in JSON format

{"evidence": 7, "testimony": 3}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 7,
  "testimony": 3
}

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, with nothing before or after it.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyze the spatial contradiction between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, L'Belle states that he was in the hallway, hiding in the shadows, and later fled down the hallway to his right, towards the end where the phone is located. According to Evidence 5 (Crime Scene Diagram), the phone is at the end of the hallway to the north of the juncture, and the juncture is between the dead end with the phone and the corridor leading to the Fox Chamber.

**Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 6, and 7 are relevant to the spatial arrangement described in the testimonies and evidence.

**Step 3:** Explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- Prop 1: If L'Belle was hiding in the shadows of the hallway, then he was not in plain sight.
- Prop 6: If L'Belle fled down the hallway to his right, then the phone is to the right of the hallway.
- Prop 7: If the phone is at the end of the hallway, then the hallway is to the south of the phone.

L'Belle's testimony (Testimony 0) indicates he was hiding in the shadows of the hallway, which aligns with Prop 1. However, Testimony 4 contradicts Evidence 5 and Prop 7. According to Evidence 5, the phone is at the northern end of the hallway, meaning the hallway would be to the south of the phone. But Testimony 4 states that L'Belle fled down the hallway to his right, towards the phone, implying that the phone should be to the right of the hallway, which conflicts with the orientation given in Evidence 5.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction arises from the direction L'Belle fled relative to the location of the phone as described in the Crime Scene Diagram. The diagram clearly places the phone at the northern end of the hallway, making the hallway to the south of the phone. However, L'Belle's statement about fleeing to his right towards the phone suggests the phone should be to the right of the hallway, which is inconsistent with the diagram.

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on the spatial relationships described.

Proposition 6 states: "If Metis Cykes is on the workbench side of the room then the workbench is on the same side of the room as Metis Cykes." This proposition supports the idea that the workbench and the round window are on the same side of the room, which contradicts the description given in Evidence 9.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

According to Ponco's testimony (Testimony 3), Metis Cykes fell down near the round window on the workbench side of the room, implying that both the round window and the workbench are on the same side. However, Evidence 9 indicates that the workbench is on the opposite side of the room from where the victim fell, creating a spatial contradiction.

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions relate to the staff room, the orca pool room, and the visibility of objects within these rooms.

**Step 3:** If applicable propositions exist, explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states: "If Rimes Marlon is inside the staff room at 10:10 AM then he is disconnected from the orca pool room." Proposition 3 states: "If the coins are scattered around the orca pool room then Rimes Marlon cannot see the captain or the orca from the door."

This contradicts Rimes Marlon's claim that he could not see the captain or the orca from the staff room, especially if the coins were scattered around the orca pool room.

**Step 4:** If none of the listed propositions apply, explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

While Propositions 1 and 3 support Rimes Marlon's statements, the spatial reasoning here involves understanding that the security footage from the visitor's corridor would allow a clear view into the orca pool room, regardless of whether Rimes Marlon was in the staff room or not. Therefore, if the coins were scattered around the orca pool room, it would be possible to see them from the visitor's corridor, suggesting that Rimes Marlon's statement about not seeing the captain or the orca due to the coins is inconsistent with the potential visibility from the visitor's corridor.

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

Shih-na's testimonies indicate her presence and actions during the fires. Specifically, she mentions being inside Babahl's office during the first fire (Testimony 0), searching for the Yatagarasu in the next room (Testimony 1), hearing a scream from the next room (Testimony 2), and being unable to see the Yatagarasu (Testimony 3). These testimonies suggest a sequence of events and locations.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify a contradiction based on Shih-na's movements and observations.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, and 4 are relevant to Shih-na's location and visibility:
- Prop 1: If Shih-na is inside Babahl's office during the first fire, then she is disconnected from Allebahst's office.
- Prop 3: If Shih-na hears a scream from the next room over, then she is outside of the room where the scream originated.
- Prop 4: If Shih-na is unable to catch a glimpse of the Yatagarasu, then the Yatagarasu is behind an obstruction from her viewpoint.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Based on the testimonies:
- Testimony 0 states Shih-na was inside Babahl's office during the first fire.
- Testimony 2 states she heard a scream from the next room over, implying she was outside the room where the scream came from.
- Testimony 3 states she could not see the Yatagarasu, suggesting there was an obstruction.

Combining these with Propositions 1, 3, and 4, we find that:
- According to Prop 1, if Shih-na was inside Babahl's office, she would be disconnected from Allebahst's office.
- According to Prop 3, if she heard a scream from the next room, she must have been outside that room.
- According to Prop 4, if she could not see the Yatagarasu, the Yatagarasu must have been behind an obstruction.

However, the contradiction arises when we consider the spatial relationship implied by Testimony 2 and Testimony 3 together with the evidence of the Revolving Fireplace Wall (Evidence 29):
- Testimony 2 suggests Shih-na was outside the room where the scream originated.
- Testimony 3 indicates she could not see the Yatagarasu due to an obstruction.
- Evidence 29 states the Yatagarasu escaped through the revolving fireplace wall, which implies the wall was used as a means of movement between rooms.

If the Yatagarasu escaped through the wall, it would mean the wall was not a permanent obstruction, thus contradicting the idea that Shih-na could not see the Yatagarasu due to a fixed obstruction.
